# Decisions log

Decisions taken on the founder's behalf by the product-owner agent during the autonomous
build (`docs/notes/autonomous-build.md`). Newest first. Each entry: date · module ·
decision · reason · spec ID.

## Where the build stopped

Phase 1 ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at 31c1923; Phase 2 at e060f98 (phase2-acceptance.md); Phase 3 (6.15a) at 397c1e8 (phase3-acceptance.md); 6.12 at 639d06b; 6.14 increment 1 at 7b92b98; 6.13 Part A ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at e5e38e0; 6.14 increment 2 ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES at 8e3aa33 (614b-C1..C6). Now: STOPPED for handover to a local CLI (founder, 2026-10-04); see docs/notes/handoff-local-cli.md. 6.13 Part B: Run 0 and Run 1a accepted; Run 1b phase A in progress, tip cdec150 is WIP and NOT CHECKED; then runs 2-5 under the founder's one-review process. 6.16, 6.17 and Part B acceptance are in docs/notes/backlog.md.

**Environment caveat for the founder.** This session ran on PostgreSQL 16 (no Docker) and
could not download Prisma's native schema engine (binaries.prisma.sh is blocked by the
session's network policy). Migrations were generated and applied with Prisma's npm-hosted
WASM schema engine (same engine version, e922089) through `@prisma/adapter-pg`, and every
migration folder was proved by rebuilding a throwaway database from it and diffing
against `schema.prisma` (empty diff). Before relying on them, run `npx prisma migrate
dev` on a normal machine against Postgres 17 and confirm it reports no drift.

**Resolved 2026-10-01 (local machine).** With the native Prisma 7.9.1 engine on PostgreSQL
17.10, `prisma migrate deploy` applied all 23 migration folders (through
`20261001011312_phase1_closeout`) to a fresh throwaway database with none failed or rolled
back, and `prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource --to-schema prisma/schema.prisma`
reported an empty migration (exit 0). The migrations are good on the native toolchain.

- 2026-10-04 · Local machine · Native-engine check PASSED, and the dev database rebuilt (founder's choice). On the founder's Windows machine (native Prisma 7.9.1, PostgreSQL 17.10 in Docker): `prisma migrate deploy` applied all 153 folders to a fresh throwaway database (`perolehan_native_check`) with none failed; `prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource --to-schema prisma/schema.prisma` reported an empty migration; every one of the 211 CHECK names the migrations add is in pg_constraint (213 in all), all 17 partial unique indexes are in pg_indexes, and StandingEvent_ends_after_start, StandingRequest_withdrawal and ExerciseParty_exerciseId_vendorId_key are present. This clears the native-engine item every acceptance carried (Phase 3 condition 1; 6.12, 6.13 Part A, 6.14). The local dev database was at 23 migrations; applying the other 130 to a copy of it stopped at 20261004100301_contract_matter, whose pre-check correctly refused an old-demo CommitteeDecision with matterKind CONTRACT and changed nothing. Founder's choice (asked): the old database kept untouched as `perolehan_backup_20261004` (it holds, besides demo rows, 1 Person and 14 AuditEntry rows not created by the build); a fresh `perolehan` built from all 153 folders, seeded, demo loaded. Typecheck needs more than the 8 GB default heap since run 1b C3 (the cloud used 12.5 GB): locally `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=14000`. A stale `.next/dev/types` from an old dev run still named the removed /reports page and was cleared. At cdec150 (the WIP C8) `npm run check` passes (docs/notes/cloud-build/evidence/gates/r1b-local-00-check.txt).

- 2026-10-04 · 6.13 Part B · Run 1a acceptance and rulings BD-613-127..131 (product owner, read-only review, at 1d94a3e; residual round 51a90b1..7aae728 and its entry). **Run 1a ACCEPTED WITH CARRIES**, covering the build, the fix round and the residual round. Run 1b starts once the parallel scoped code review of 51a90b1..1d94a3e is clean. Anything that review raises is fixed in one commit flagged against run 1a, with a red first and its gate file, and does not reopen Run 1a. Reproduced: tree clean at 1d94a3e; src/lib/contract plus part-b-provenance, part-b-settings and evaluation/lock-order give 29 files / 495 tests green. Gate files r1ar-01..07 are present; r1ar-07 ends with exit 0. Full gates are left to the review. Read: certificateVoidAct, actStatusFault, recordOutcome's VARIATION gate (src/app/committees/actions.ts), the ContractType model, part-b-provenance.test.ts's PART_A_SHARED, and the note appended under the fix-round entry.
  - Conditions met: BD-613-124 (four columns, each nullable with no DEFAULT; pre-check first; empty diff; the rule is read from the model and has a self-test); BD-613-123 (the gate-reach scan with its self-test, and the failure it found, 68 < 83, was fixed rather than waived); S-1, S-2, S-3 (a)–(d), S-5; and the fix-round over-claims, corrected by a note appended under that entry rather than by editing it. New-this-round durable gate files: met. Text snapshot: 1,427 routes with an empty diff. The verify table, 8 of 8, covers the states the snapshot cannot show.
  - BD-613-127 CONFIRMED as built, with one condition added. Voiding an unissued certificate reads the CERTIFICATE row, so no draft is stranded when its own family closes. The proposal as written has a dead end. On a terminated contract, an interim draft is refused by CERTIFICATE and also by its own pre-issue family. Run 5's close would then refuse forever while that draft is neither issued nor void. Condition for run 4, before any act writes TERMINATED: in TERMINATED, voiding an unissued certificate of any kind is allowed by the table. Only the certificate's own void rules can refuse it, and they name themselves. Test: every kind × every unissued status at TERMINATED, the void is ok or refused by its own rule. A termination is never blocked because drafts exist. Run 5's closureFaults refuse the close while any certificate is neither issued nor void, naming each. CANCELLED keeps everything as it was, and no close leaves it. CLOSED stays frozen, which is safe because no draft can reach it once run 5's refusal is built.
  - BD-613-128: ContractType's convention is ACCEPTED for now, and the type is not split piecemeal. The row has about fifteen behaviour columns under one `provenance`, which has been its accepted 6.12 convention. Splitting only the three typed twins would leave their label twins (eotInstrument, completionInstrument, claimsApprovalCommitteeCode) under the row tag, which is incoherent. Conditions: (1) wherever a screen shows the type's tag, it is labelled as the source of the contract type as a whole and is never placed beside a single value. (2) claimsCommitteeId is OD-B20, an RSAJ choice. When run 2 gives it a reader, the claims screen must not show the type's tag as the committee's source. It shows the committee, or "Not set", with no tag. (3) Held for the founder's end review: should ContractType move to one provenance per behaviour column? If so, it is one flagged commit against 6.12 covering every behaviour column, carried over under the Part A rule below.

Each gets a self-test. Any new uncovered site it finds is fixed in that commit, not waived.
  - BD-613-131 APPROVED in part. DEFERRED passes the VARIATION gate, because it records nothing on the variation. Every other outcome on a variation's matter stays gated. Do not do what the round proposed: refuse TERMINATED while a variation matter is pending. Termination is the employer's contractual remedy and must not wait on a committee sitting. Instead: (1) the acts that write FINAL_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED and CLOSED refuse while a variation matter is pending at any sitting, naming the matter and its sitting. (2) For TERMINATED, run 4 proves with a test that a sitting holding such a matter can still be minuted, by deferral or withdrawal under the committee's own rules. (3) The sitting's outcome drawer states the table's refusal for the gated outcomes, in the act's words, which fixes self-review (a)'s weak spot. (4) The cancelled refusal gains its Malay. All four are built in the run that first writes FCI or TERMINATED, before that write.

A notice of intention issued while active cannot be despatched or acknowledged once the contract is practically complete. *Blocks:* run 4's termination after PC. Run 4 either allows the case in PC for an intention issued before it, or refuses the CPC while an intention is undelivered, naming it. Both orders get tests.
    - voidFaults refuses VOID_WOULD_UNDO_STATUS for every issued PRACTICAL_COMPLETION and MAKING_GOOD_DEFECTS certificate, a sectional one included, though only the last scope's issue writes the status. *Blocks:* run 2 (sections). voidFaults takes whether the issue wrote the status, not the kind alone.

Each goes in its own commit, flagged against accepted Part A. Unlike Part B's, Part A's editors already write these, so the split must decide how a set value is carried. Copying it to each new column restates one tag as two, which is a ruling to make, not an assumption. *Blocks:* Post-award controls showing an honest tag per value (today one STATED tag vouches for both a basis and a base), and emptying part-b-provenance.test.ts's PART_A_SHARED list.
    - withdrawVersion on a closed contract: the document store's withdrawal of a version is document-wide and does not read the owner's table row, so a version attached to a closed contract's record can be withdrawn. Note only, as directed. *Blocks:* nothing in Part B's runs. The document store's owner of the version decides.

Polymorphic nullable owners sit outside the act-status scan (BD-613-130). A sitting cannot be minuted while a variation matter's outcome is refused (BD-613-131).
  - **Build decisions for ruling (BD-613-127..131).**
    - **BD-613-127 (S-1, the lead's decision).** Voiding an unissued certificate reads the CERTIFICATE row, as before the fix round, so no draft whose own family closes is stranded (an interim at FCI, an advance at PC, an EOT at MGC; verify S-1a, S-1b, S-2a). Consequences for the product owner:
      - (1) Where CERTIFICATE refuses every certificate act (cancelled, terminated, closed), an unissued certificate cannot be voided. A draft left when a contract is terminated or closed stays non-VOID.
      - (2) A draft TERMINATION_INTENT, TERMINATION or TERMINATED_FINAL on a terminated contract can be signed (its family allows TERMINATED) but not voided.
      - Neither can happen before run 4.
      - Proposed: confirm. Run 4 lets an unissued certificate's void read CERTIFICATE or its pre-issue family, whichever allows, with a test for the termination's kinds. Run 5's closureFaults refuse the close while any certificate is neither issued nor void, naming each.
    - **BD-613-128 (BD-613-124's reach).** The rule "every Part B setting value column has its own provenance" is built over PostAwardPolicy, where Part B's settings live. ContractType's three typed twins carry the type row's single `provenance`, which is the contract type's convention (one tag per row) since 6.12. Proposed: accept the type's convention for its twins, or split them with run 1b (eotInstrumentKind) and run 2 (completionVariant, claimsCommitteeId) as each first gets a reader.
    - **BD-613-129 (the gate-reach scan's exceptions).** Four components a gated section may render are not gates:
      - OwnerAttachments, which the store judges (BD-613-115);
      - Tabs, DataList and MoreText, which record nothing.
      - Each is named with its reason and must be reached, so none goes stale.
      - Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-130 (S-3c's reach).**
      - Writers are found among `function` declarations (`export const f = async () =>` is not read; none writes an owned model today).
      - Ownership is still S3's required chain. IssuedCopy and RoutingCheck (reached by nullable, either-or links to a contract or certificate) and CommitteeDecision (a nullable link to a variation or matter) are not contract-owned.
      - Proposed: carry a widening of ownership to those polymorphic links, in its own flagged commit, before run 4 writes TERMINATED.
    - **BD-613-131 (S-3d's consequence).** recordOutcome on a variation's matter refuses at FCI, terminated and closed, DEFERRED included, because it goes through the same act. A sitting cannot be minuted with a matter pending (MATTERS_PENDING), so a sitting holding such a matter could not be closed. This cannot happen before runs 4 and 5.
      - Proposed: DEFERRED passes the gate, since it records nothing on the variation. Run 4 and run 5's writers of TERMINATED, FCI and CLOSED refuse while a variation matter is pending at a sitting, naming it.
      - The other sitting acts on such a matter (votes, recusal, withdrawal) are left to the committee's own rules. The sitting's outcome drawer says what the act would, (a) above.

  - **Process.**
    - No `pkill -f`. The dev servers were started in the background with their PIDs in the scratchpad's dev-3100.pid, and each was stopped by that PID and then `fuser -k 3100/tcp` (the npx PID leaves the next-server child on the port). No server ran on 3000.
    - Heavy steps ran one at a time: check, then suite, then verify.sh, seed, load, server, harness, snapshot.
    - Every commit staged explicit paths and ends with the two lines.

    - The contract page's terminated or closed banner builds its finding by hand (`actStatusText({ code: status === "TERMINATED" ? … })`, page.tsx:158) rather than asking `actStatusFault` for a family. *Blocks:* nothing today (the words are the table's). A family allowed in TERMINATED or CLOSED could make the banner's general words read as if everything were refused. Fix: compose the banner from the families the state allows.
    - statusCalloutTitle for MAKING_GOOD_COMPLETE and FINAL_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED reads "The contract is making good of defects complete" and "The contract is final certificate issued". *Blocks:* nothing until a callout is shown in those states (run 2 writes MGC, run 5 FCI). It is already on run 2's carry list ("statusCalloutTitle checked for each state shown").
    - MONEY_NEEDS_APPROVED_ROUTE's words on a kind that is not money-bearing. *Blocks:* nothing reachable: only money-bearing kinds raise it, and S5's three kinds now have their own words. Fix: name the kind's own reason if a non-money kind ever raises it.
    - contractStage has no per-section DLP and no third-party rectification of its own. *Blocks:* run 2 (sections and their defects); contractStage reads one DLP for the contract.
    - A committee's decision on a terminated contract's variation matter does not read the table (src/app/committees/actions.ts writes CommitteeDecision, a nullable link, outside S3's required-chain scope). *Blocks:* nothing until a contract can be terminated (run 4). Fix: the committee act asks VARIATION's row for a variation matter, and the scan widens to nullable links of a contract matter's kind.
  - **Build decisions for ruling (BD-613-120..126).**
    - **BD-613-120 (named in f6b6409).** After their issue the termination's own certificates (TERMINATION_INTENT, TERMINATION, TERMINATED_FINAL) read their case or the terminated final payment's route, not CERTIFICATE. CERTIFICATE is refused in TERMINATED, so the termination's notice could not be despatched or acknowledged once its own issue wrote TERMINATED. Proposed: confirm.
    - **BD-613-121 (S5's reach).** The review asked that the three matter-approved kinds fail closed "with no routing type". They are refused from routing too, because a routing type's route is not their approval of record, which is the matter's own route (plan, run 1b "Time"). Run 1b's matter hook replaces the refusal with the matter's answer. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-122 (the award's securities register reads FORMATION).** Lodging and checking a security or policy on the contract reads FORMATION's row (to the final certificate; refused once terminated or closed), not a family of its own, so the count stays 35. Claims and releases keep their own families (BOND_CLAIM, SECURITY_RELEASE, DESIGN_BOND_RELEASE). Proposed: approve, or name a SECURITIES family (36).
    - **BD-613-123 (the panel gate is a context).** `ActGate` passes a section's refusal to every RecordEditor and Blocked inside it, and both shared components put it before their own blocker. That covers drawers with no blocker of their own without touching 83 editors. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-124 (S7's scope).** Only the two shared columns the review named are split. terminationProvenance still covers the re-tender cap and the loss-days switch, and designBondProvenance the bond's rate and years. Proposed: split each with the run that builds its editor (4 and 2), as S7 did here.
    - **BD-613-125 (the instruction's plans read VARIATION).** INSTRUCTION_SKETCH (a variation's instruction, not the contract) also reads the table, through VARIATION's row. Its plans freeze once the final certificate is issued, or the contract is terminated or closed, as every variation act does. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-126 (contractStage's not-set marker only at practical completion).** The DLP is named not set only where the stage turns on it (PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE). From MAKING_GOOD_COMPLETE on, the period is over by the CMGD's issue. Proposed: approve.

  - **Process.**
    - No `pkill -f`.
    - The dev servers were started in the background, each PID written to the scratchpad's dev-3100.pid (or dev-3000.pid). Each was stopped by that PID and then `fuser -k <port>/tcp`, because `kill` of the npx PID left the next-server child on the port. One server on 3000 from an earlier session, with no PID file, was stopped by `fuser -k 3000/tcp` at the start.
    - A probe test started with `vitest --root /` began scanning the filesystem. It was stopped by its three PIDs (kill <pid>, never by pattern) and re-run inside the repository.
    - Setup edits, all on perolehan_verify only:
      - demo_con3's status set by SQL to TERMINATED, CLOSED and PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE and back, because no writer of those states exists before run 2;
      - demo_ob5's frozen finding written by SQL (C1);
      - a CONTRACT matter inserted and rolled back (PRE);
      - while investigating 110b between runs, the VARIATION_APPLICATION setting cleared and restored by SQL on the verify database, which was then rebuilt before the final run.
    - perolehan was never hand-edited. The migrations reached it only through pmig deploy.
    - Lint ran with every check; no commit was red.
  - **Carries from the Run 0 re-review's Considers (process change 3: logged, each with what it blocks).**
    - C1 was promoted and built (b261330); C3 is BD-613-110, built (09c4572); C5 was built (d8784b2).
    - **C2.** `cancellationEnded` compares registeredOn with effectiveOn. The new column `cancelsRegistrationOn` describes the same fact another way. They agree only under the invariants: effectiveOn is never before the registration day or after today, and only a NEW approval moves registeredOn. Comparing `registeredOn !== cancelsRegistrationOn` would make one source of truth and retire CANCELLED_APPROVE_FROM. *Blocks:* any future act that moves registeredOn other than a NEW approval, or allows a cancellation effective before the registration day. Such an act must take this change first. Nothing in Part B does.
    - **C4.** The Malay of `cancellationWords` and CANCELLED_NEW_APPLICATION_ONLY prints the body's English label, because only `labelEn` is frozen on the event while `RegistrationBody.labelMs` exists. *Blocks:* nothing on screen (the screens render English). It does block any Malay output that quotes the body (a notice or a letter), which is not built. Fix: freeze `bodyLabelMsSnapshot` beside it, falling back to the English.
    - **C6.** The withdraw drawer also shows on an ended cancellation, where its "registers again" advice is moot, and withdrawing such a record leaves registeredOn at the re-registration day without saying so. *Blocks:* nothing; no false fact. Words only: the drawer and the audit should say the registration day stays.
    - **C7.** Older cancellation rows hold the stored English "Cancelled by …" text that is no longer read. *Blocks:* nothing (not read). Optional one-line data migration blanking reasonSnapshot for REGISTRATION_CANCELLED rows (none outside demo and verify).
  - **BD-613-103 carry-overs:** none remain. BD-613-103, 105(e) and 106 were built in the fix round (bcd02d5, 1862ae7, f41bbb0); the plan names no other.
  - **Build decisions for ruling (BD-613-112..119).**
    - **BD-613-112 (the B4 enum values not in run 1a).** Built in run 1a: the eleven CertificateKind values (the kinds' maps) and the five ContractStatus values (ACT_STATUS, contractStage), each alone. Moved: ContractClock ×12, BaselineReason ×2, PendingDeductionKind ×2 and AttachmentPurpose ×11 go, each still alone in its migration, with the run whose acts or attachment owners use them. Reason: a clock value appears at once as a settable row on Configuration › Post-award with nothing reading it, and an attachment purpose needs its owner column in the rebuilt owner CHECKs, whose tables do not exist before their run. Either would be a setting or a value that does nothing. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-113 (CLOSE in TERMINATED).** BD-613-99 lists seven acts allowed in TERMINATED, and the table holds exactly those, so CLOSE is refused in TERMINATED. ADR 0031's transition TERMINATED → CLOSED then has no act that can take it. Proposed: add CLOSE to TERMINATED's exceptions (once the terminated account is settled and the closure's preconditions hold, run 5), or rule that a terminated contract is closed through the termination case. Run 1a builds the table as ruled; the transition stays unreachable until ruled.
    - **BD-613-114 (whose acts a termination's certificates read).** `certificateAct`: the TERMINATION and TERMINATION_INTENT certificates read TERMINATION_CASE, and TERMINATED_FINAL reads TERMINATED_FINAL_ROUTE; every other kind reads CERTIFICATE. That is how "the termination case" covers its own notices once TERMINATED is written (the notice's distribution, the guarantor's copy). Proposed: confirm.
    - **BD-613-115 (attachments and states after Active).** Attaching a document to a contract's records is judged by the document store's owner rules, not by ACT_STATUS. Run 1a only made "executed" there read isExecuted. The site-record purposes (diary photo, pipe log, as-built) open only in ACTIVE, so in a later state they would read "kept from possession of site". That is wrong but unreachable, because no state after Active can be written before run 2. Proposed: run 2 decides them with the first writer, and CLOSED freezes a contract's papers.
    - **BD-613-116 (editors for the settings and twins).** The thirteen PostAwardPolicy settings and the three ContractType twins are in the schema, unset, with nothing reading them. Each gets its Configuration editor, words and History labels with the run that reads it (1b: weather guidance, the waiver switch, the anti-oppression days, eotInstrumentKind, claimsCommitteeId; 2: design bond, releases, completionVariant; 3: the nominated settings; 4: re-tender cap and composition; 5: final basis). Reason: an editor for a setting nothing reads invites a value that does nothing. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-117 (the contract-matter domains and the /approvals lock line).** CONTRACT is record-tabled now. The contract-matter domains (eot, contract_period_extension, lad_waiver, termination, nominated_increase, loss_expense, and the nomination's) join RECORD_TABLED_DOMAINS, and /approvals gains its ContractMatter lock line (reading ACT_STATUS), with run 1b's send act, when such routes first exist. Today "eot" is still tabled at a sitting under another kind (labels.test.ts asserts it), and no contract matter has a route. Proposed: approve.
    - **BD-613-118 (the nomination letter on the needs list).** Intake 2 says "NOMINATION_LETTER per part under OD-B19's PER_LETTER". Run 1a lists the kind once (one need through CertificateKindSetting), because the letter parts and the PER_LETTER storage are run 3's. Proposed: run 3 lists one need per part when the switch is PER_LETTER, unset showing the switch's own need.
    - **BD-613-119 (the table's rows for the states after Active).** The builder decided these from B5 and ADR 0031, for ruling:
      - Before Part B's families: FORMATION, COPIES_AND_CUSTODY, CERTIFICATE and RETENTION_RELEASE are let through to their own rules up to FINAL_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED. BASELINE, SUPERVISION, VALUATION, DAMAGES, VARIATION and SUM_ADJUSTMENT are let through up to MAKING_GOOD_COMPLETE. OPENING_POSITION and ADVANCE_PAYMENT stop at ACTIVE.
      - Part B's families: never before execution. EOT, PERIOD_EXTENSION, COMPLETION, DEFECTS and MAKING_GOOD run to PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE. LOSS_EXPENSE, LAD_WAIVER, NOMINATED and COMPLETION_OPENING run to MAKING_GOOD_COMPLETE. SECURITY_RELEASE runs in PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE to FINAL_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED. DESIGN_BOND_RELEASE runs from PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE, CLOSED included. FINAL_ACCOUNT runs in PRACTICALLY_COMPLETE (where no DLP applies, judged by its own rule) and MAKING_GOOD_COMPLETE. FINAL_PAYMENT and CLOSE run in FINAL_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED.
      - A refusal in a state with no word of its own reads "Not recorded while the contract stands at “{state}”." (EN and MS).
      - Each run that builds an act re-reads its row and changes it in its own flagged commit if its acts need otherwise.

- 2026-10-04 · 6.13 Part B · Product owner rulings on the Run 0 fix round BD-613-108..111 and the process changes from Run 1a on (product owner, read-only review, at e53b3d2; 7614388..e53b3d2, the fix-round entry, rulings BD-613-103..107). Run 0 fix round ACCEPTED WITH CONDITIONS; run 1a may start; these rulings stand subject to the parallel code review's findings. Gates reproduced at e53b3d2: 527 files / 8,307 tests, typecheck, lint with check-ui clean, tree clean. Checked: cancellationEnded (standing.ts) is strict and the only rule; cancelledSupplierIds (repo.ts) filters through it; way-back.test.ts covers the boundary day, the day after, send and approval separately, and both orders with a withdrawal; both migrations check existing data first, and the index is partial as stated. Rulings:
  - BD-613-108 CONFIRMED as built: (a) strict "after", with the same-day send and approval refused naming the next day; (b) the kind asked at open, send and approval; (c) a blacklisting stops the new registration's approval; (d) an ended cancellation stays on the record, marked ended, and can still be withdrawn as recorded in error; (e) one rule in code for the standing and the barred list; (f) registeredOn moves to the approval day, with the old day kept in the audit.
  - Condition (first doc commit of run 1a): ADR 0019 gains an Amendment 2 recording (a)–(f) and BD-613-109. Each claim cites its test (way-back.test.ts, one-live-cancellation.test.ts, standing-order.test.ts). Amendment 1's single line "lifted by a new registration" is today the only written statement of a mechanism with six parts.
  - No waiting period between a body's cancellation and a new registration is built. Questions to RSAJ add: whether one applies after a registration body's cancellation. If yes, it becomes a setting, left unset.
  - BD-613-109 APPROVED as built: unique on (supplierId, cancelsRegistrationOn) for live cancellations. The alternative (the literal index, with approval stamping the cancellation ended) is rejected: it adds a second writer to an append-only fact.
  - BD-613-110 APPROVED for run 1a, in its own commit flagged against accepted 6.13 Part A, after BD-613-103's carry-overs and before ACT_STATUS:
    - check the variation application's form through raiseFormFault when the prior approval is sent, and again at its last step, refusing in EN and MS with the configured location words;
    - then openCertificate refuses a missing templateId;
    - demo_hct5 stays the historical case, with its words unchanged;
    - tests cover both orders (the form cleared between send and last step; the form set after a refused send) and assert that the last step and openCertificate agree.

    Reason for doing it now: today a variation whose form is unset at that step is left with "no way to give it a form yet". The words are honest, but a dead end on the route that decides variations is not left for later.
  - BD-613-111 APPROVED: only bid openings are listed; a refused sale writes nothing, and the words say none can be listed. Recording refused sale attempts has no requirement and is not built.
  - Process slips (lint red from e2d13fa to 601e017; one `pkill -f`): recorded, no history rewritten.

  - Process changes from run 1a:
    - (1) ENDORSED, AMENDED. The self-review is a section of the run's decisions-log entry, not a separate file. Each class gets evidence: a test name, file:line, or a quoted command result. "None applies" is allowed only with the reason. Two classes are added to the six listed:
      - codes never on screen and money never a float;
      - an ADR or plan changed whenever a mechanism it describes changes (the BD-613-108 gap above).
    - (2) ENDORSED, AMENDED. The both-orders and screen–act agreement tests are written first and shown failing in the builder's evidence (the red output quoted). They are committed together with the fix, so no commit is red.
    - (3) ENDORSED, AMENDED. Fix rounds take Must and Should only. A Consider is logged as a carry, with what it blocks, unless the product owner promotes it. The code reviewer files as Should, not Consider, any finding that touches an authority limit, an approver, money, a false exit or an unset value shown as set, or a scan's blind spot.
    - (4) ENDORSED, AMENDED. For every run that adds or changes an act or a state transition (1b, 2 and after), the code reviewer checks a short design note before any screen is built. The note gives each act's inputs, its refusals with EN/MS words, the both-orders table, the lock order and the single-writer scans. Screens wait until its Musts are answered.
    - (5) ENDORSED, AMENDED. One script, `npm run check` (typecheck then lint), runs before every commit. The full suite still runs before every commit that changes a shared rule's output. The entry reports the gates per commit.

  - BD-613-108 (M-1, the lead's decision as built, for the product owner to confirm): the way back is a NEW application for a supplier whose registration is CANCELLED; its approval resets registeredOn; standing() treats the cancellation as ended when registeredOn is after its effectiveOn; RENEWAL and UPGRADE are refused while CANCELLED, naming the exit. Build decisions inside it: (a) "after" is strict, so a new registration approved on the very day the cancellation took effect would not end it; sending and approving a NEW then are refused (CANCELLED_APPROVE_FROM), naming the next day; (b) the kind is asked at open, send and approval, so a renewal opened before the cancellation is not approved under it, and a NEW opened under a cancellation later withdrawn as recorded in error is refused (KIND_NOT_FOR_STATUS: the officer rejects it, which leaves the supplier REGISTERED); (c) a blacklisting beside the cancellation lets a NEW open (as for an applicant today) but stops its approval (BLACKLISTED_NOT_APPROVED); (d) an ended cancellation stays on the record, marked "Ended: registered again on …" with a CANCELLATION_ENDED note, and may still be withdrawn as recorded in error; (e) the barred count and Standing list name cancelled suppliers by the same rule in code (cancelledSupplierIds), since a query cannot compare the event's day with the supplier's; (f) registeredOn moves to the new approval day (the old day kept in the audit), so a later suspension or cancellation may not predate the new registration.
  - BD-613-109 (the consider's index, adapted): the index as asked (unique on supplierId for live cancellations) would, with M-1, refuse a supplier's next genuine cancellation for ever, because an ended cancellation is kept and not withdrawn. Built instead: each cancellation names the registration it cancels (cancelsRegistrationOn, the registeredOn when recorded, CHECK, backfilled), and the partial unique index is on (supplierId, cancelsRegistrationOn); a registered supplier with no registration day is refused (REGISTRATION_DAY_NOT_RECORDED, unreachable through the product). Alternative for ruling: the literal index, with the new registration's approval stamping the cancellation ended (a second writer on an append-only table).
  - BD-613-110 (S-6's check): one product path legitimately opens a certificate without a form: the variation's application (VO-660) at its prior approval's last step, on the form configured then, "unset: none yet" (BD-613-52, route-write.ts openApplication). So openCertificate does not refuse a missing templateId (it would refuse the approver's last step), and that kind's words do not advise "void it and raise it again", which would be false: the product never raises it again once void. Proposed, flagged against Part A, for run 1a or later: check the application's form when the prior approval is sent (raiseFormFault, as every raise) and again at the last step; then make openCertificate refuse a missing templateId, leaving demo_hct5 the historical case.
  - BD-613-111 (S-4's list): only bid openings can be listed as judgments made while a cancellation stood (each opened bid freezes the register's finding); a sale refused for it writes nothing (registerAtSale refuses before the sale is recorded), so the words say none can be listed rather than imply there were none. Proposed: keep; recording refused sale attempts has no requirement.

M-1, step 1 (70f35f5, flagged against accepted 6.14 inc 1): the first signature's pin, the certificate page's "not ready" preview and the demo call one pure function, pinCheck (pin-context.ts: pinRefusals on the certificate's own context); sheetOf and pinContextOf move there from write.ts, routesOf/checksOf to a pure sheet-rows.ts (both re-exported), so the context is drawn from a row without the database client and tested; the demo pins through it on its signing day. M-1 (bc1315a): the page said a routed certificate's form "is not ready" all through routing, advising a void, wherever a printed field was bound to the day received, the day approved or the elapsed time, which are empty until the route is approved. The pin's context now carries the sheet as STARTED (whether its route is approved: the approved day set or the latest route approved; its type; its target's state; its values in English and Malay exactly as the issue prints them) or UNJUDGED; sheetValueFaults judges the type and the target once the sheet has started, the two days and the elapsed time only once approved, each in both languages as printedBlankFaults holds it at issue. New findings, EN and MS, not FIELD_NO_VALUE's wording and never "void": SHEET_VALUE_EMPTY (record it on the Routing sheet tab, or mark the field completed by hand), SHEET_TARGET_NOT_SET (names the routing sheet type, why it has no target for this certificate, where a type's targets are set, and that this certificate keeps the type it started with, so the exit is the form's field before the first signature), SHEET_NOT_JUDGED (the time zone unset: the page says so under "Whether its form can be signed on is not judged yet", rather than skipping the checks the act makes). Consider (9253a1a, flagged against accepted 6.14 inc 1, stricter): the certificate scan read no raw call with a nested type argument ($queryRaw<Array<Entry>>`…` failed the whole match); the argument now runs to the backtick or parenthesis, with a self-test; RAW_ALLOW gains the alias in-use read by its exact text. S-2 and consider (b8705bd): aliasesKeeping drops an alias the edit made canonical (one already canonical is still refused as a slip); removedAliases leaves out a wording that stays canonical, so no reason is asked; the save's reason is capped at 2,000 (and the correction save's, the same uncapped read); the in-use check runs in the database (wholeWordPattern: wordAt's whole-word rule as a Postgres expression, case ignored), selecting only the certificates' numbers, 10 named and "and N more". S-1 and consider (8cc32b3): every /documents tab again carries "N routing sheets are past their target", the header's "(N past the target, M not judged)" and, with the time zone unset, "(not judged: the time zone is not set)"; the open sheets are read through a slim select (only the columns a sheet is drawn from) on contracts not cancelled (M-2), judged whole, and the Routing sheets tab pages the same set; while a search is active the Library badge is the match count, as the list and pager; with no search the Library's page reuses the register's count. Not built, logged as a go-live performance note: a pg_trgm GIN index on CertificateDocument.renderedText (and AwardDocument.renderedText) for the Library's ILIKE search, once a customer's register is large enough for a sequential scan to show; the in-use check's regular expression scans the same column and would want it too. The whole-word match in Postgres uses [:alnum:], which follows the database's character type (C.UTF-8 here: accented letters are letters); under a plain C locale an accented letter would not count as one, so the check could warn where wordAt would not (it errs towards asking for a reason).   - BD-614-23 APPROVED. FR-CRT-012's refusal stays at the first signature and at issue, not at raise: a draft may be raised and routed while document control writes the text (OD-2 A). Designing, reviewing and issuing the five missing forms is RSAJ's (B6, a go-live condition). Depends on the fix-now items: formNeedFault really shared by the needs list, the callout and Part B's raise; a draft with no form shows the not-ready callout. Spec: FR-CRT-012, OD-2 A.
  - BD-614-24 APPROVED; answers BD-613-43. KIND_FIGURE_KEYS stays empty. It lists what the product can fill, not customer policy, so it is a product constant. The owning module adds a kind's keys when a customer's form must print a figure. Until then a form naming a figure is refused at the first signature, never printed blank. Spec: FR-CRT-002, AGENTS.md rule 2.
  - BD-614-25 APPROVED (divergence from plan §2.2): Term (code, wording in English and Malay, at least one) + TermAlias. {{term:code}} needs a code and a wording per language. Ships empty (OD-5 A; seed checked). A term cited by a form's text cannot be removed; the race on that check is a fix-now item. Plan §2.2 and FR-CRT-011's row to be amended to match. Spec: FR-CRT-011, OD-5 A.
  - BD-614-26 APPROVED WITH AMENDMENT. Corrections never move the form's version, so the form code and revision stay unchanged (FR-CRT-010). Because two certificates at the same form version may then differ, each pin and each issue must record which corrections applied (id, wrong and right wording), not only how many (fix-now). The fidelity line's count stays. Nothing ships; each correction is document control's act (FR-CRT-010 note). Spec: FR-CRT-010, ADR 0008.
  - BD-614-27 APPROVED. The page count and printed fields are pinned with the form and move a certificate form's version. A template used by another module takes no printed fields. Spec: FR-CRT-001, OD-2 A.
  - BD-614-28 APPROVED. A printed field is either bound to a named value or completed by hand (printed blank, never keyed in the portal). A bound field the certificate cannot fill refuses the first signature; the blank-at-issue defect is a fix-now item. Choosing "by hand" for a value that exists in the system would breach FR-CRT-002; that is the configurer's choice, labelled on screen. Spec: FR-CRT-002.
  - BD-614-29 APPROVED. A routing sheet freezes its type's printed sheet and labels when it starts. A sheet started before increment 2 says none was recorded. Spec: FR-CRT-007, ADR 0008.
  - BD-614-30 APPROVED. /documents covers issued certificates, frozen award documents, certificates' copies and certificates on routing sheets; drafts, contract copies and the pre-award sheet stay where they live, and the screen says so. Depends on fix-now: cancelled contracts' items, Library loading every award document, the Document column width, whole-word term search, /documents in the sealed-figure BROADCAST scans.
  - BD-614-31 APPROVED; amends the rulings' 6.14 condition. "Shows a Callout naming 6.15b" now reads "a Callout naming the vendor portal (sign-in) in words". No module numbers on screen (inc 1 S-3; AGENTS.md rule 5 in spirit). phase3-6-rulings.md 6.14 to be amended to match.
  - BD-614-32 APPROVED. The sidebar's Reports entry, its Insight group and /reports are removed, following the founder's 2026-10-04 removal of 6.17. A not-built page promising a removed module would overstate the product. app/reports/ may stay in the scans' broadcast lists (not weakened); /documents is added (fix-now).
  - BD-614-33 CHOSEN: keep the replaced wording as an alias. Build before acceptance. Changing a term's wording keeps each replaced wording (per language) as an alias of the same term, in the same audited transaction. If the old wording already belongs to another term, the edit is refused, naming that term. Removing an alias that an issued certificate's frozen text still contains warns, and asks for a reason. Test: an issued certificate is still found by its old wording after the term's wording changes. Why: FR-CRT-011's acceptance ("a search for either variant finds the record") must hold for frozen text (ADR 0008); this is the reversible option and needs no new structure. Spec: FR-CRT-011.

Add a scan test proving every entity a Configuration History button asks for is on history.ts's allow-list, so this cannot silently come back. Spec: AGENTS.md "every change is audited", rule 3.

a re-measurement needs its own `remeasurement` route at its order's value (FR-PAD-004; in the quotation regime, read from the matrix's floors, the `vo` bands). **54** the cumulative approved is every other variation's prior approval (at the S.O.'s cap, else the estimate) plus its approved increases; exempt and closed count nothing; it moves only when a value route is approved; the new sum on VO-660 = original + cumulative + this. **55** the configured basis applies to prior approval and increases (an increase's numerator is the approved so far plus the increase); agreed rates and re-measurement read their own value over the base. **56** both orders guarded where two acts touch the same figure: a cumulative basis refuses a send while another value route is pending; a revised base refuses a send while an adjustment is in progress, and an adjustment's issue while a route read over the revised sum waits (VARIATION_ROUTE_ON_BASE); an interim's issue refuses if the issued adjustments moved since it was raised (ADJUSTMENTS_MOVED); one adjustment in progress at a time and its issue refused if earlier ones moved; measurements frozen under a live order; an order on one live adjustment. **57** a variation is not closed once its prior approval is approved, a route is pending or an instruction is live. **58** the S.O.'s "not supported" goes where the `vo` domain's destination says (return → draft, terminate → closed), else the officer chooses in the same act (CO-5). **59** the S.O.'s cap is no higher than the estimate and is the prior route's value. **60** one live instruction a variation; the award's chain before formation is written as 6.11's demo writes it. **71** the demo's settings are ASSUMED: basis this variation over the original sum (OD-A2), schedule forms, first release 50%, the Variation Orders Committee's quorum 2 and simple majority. The leftover sen follows the same configured rule as the payment waterfall (OD-A4: largest remainder or first party; unset refuses only when a split leaves sen), not "the main contractor takes the rounding". No tier may go below zero: a lib invariant plus a property test that reproduces the −1 sen case. Reason: one rounding rule per product; a negative retention tier is a wrong ringgit.
  - BD-613-29 APPROVED WITH CONDITION: the subsequent minimum is judged on the amount carried forward, the first on the gross. The certificate and Configuration › Post-award say which measure applies and that it is ASSUMED (sources state no measure), so it reads as an assumption, not a rule.
  - BD-613-30 APPROVED (BD-613-13 resolved): the recommending officer is a representative with a delegation in force on the day whose scope includes valuation. Any other certificate kind resolves nobody and says so.
  - BD-613-31 APPROVED: before signing, every verified policy needs its premium receipt. This is the stricter reading; the refusal names the policy.
  - BD-613-32 APPROVED WITH CONDITION: preExecutionInterimLimit unset refuses and the screen says "not set", never "0" (gaps stay honest).

  - BD-613-39 AMENDED: see BD-613-48. The rate and the date for completion are those frozen on the non-completion decision and its CNC, not the baseline read today.
  - BD-613-40 APPROVED WITH CONDITION: a guarantee below the advance warns, not refuses (B2 open; RSAJ states coverage). The warning also shows on the CEO's approval step, not only on the contract.
  - BD-613-41 AMENDED: INTERIM_NOT_FROM_VALUATION stays as the guard, but it must have a way out. Build an opening position per contract, entered once before the first valuation the system raises: last certificate number, gross certified to date, retention held per tier, retention released, previously certified total, advance recouped by schedule number, damages charged until. Each figure needs provenance (STATED with the source certificate's reference, or ASSUMED with a reason; ASSUMED warns on every later certificate). It is written with before/after audit in the same transaction and can be corrected only with a reason, and only while no valuation has been raised from it. The core reads it as the latest issued certificate, and it must agree with any certificates not raised from a valuation (it must cover their sequence). The refusal names this way out. Must land in Part A, before acceptance. Reason: an in-flight contract stuck with no exit is a defect, not an honest gap. Spec silent; product decision, reversible.

  - BD-613-46 APPROVED WITH CONDITION: the demo figures are invented and ASSUMED, which is accepted. A contract past completion with the damages chain, an advance with recoupment, and a retention release must be in the demo before Part A acceptance (step 5 or sooner). Empty states are not evidence for acceptance.

  - BD-613-47 (code-review item 4) RULED: the signed rounding on the damages line must satisfy −RM1.00 < rounding < RM1.00, and damages + rounding ≥ 0. Enforced in src/lib (interimCertificate refuses with a named finding, with a test at ±0.99 / ±1.00) and by a CHECK on the keyed-deduction tables for kind LAD_ROUNDING. RM1.00 is the definition of a rounding to the ringgit (spec worked example: +0.20 brings 2,693.80 to 2,694.00), not a customer threshold; if RSAJ's forms round to a larger unit it becomes a setting then. "One day's rate" rejected: it would allow about RM269 of waiver without its approval route (Part B lad_waiver, example 12). The same limit applies to the ± Rounding in the Part B termination formulas.
  - BD-613-48 (damages rate source) RULED: FREEZE. decisionOf reads NonCompletion.rate and dateForCompletion as recorded with the decision and its CNC, never the baseline in force today. A decision with LAD_APPLIES and no rate refuses (RATE_NOT_SET). Where the pinned baseline's rate or date has since been revised, the certificate warns that the CNC's rate applies until a new decision and CNC are recorded; it never re-rates silently. Test: revise the baseline after an issued CNC, and the next interim still charges the CNC's rate and shows the warning. Reason: an interim must not charge a different rate than the issued certificate states (FR-LAD-002, FR-LAD-007; OD-A1 A "never rewrites an issued certificate").

Decisions (for ruling):
  - BD-613-25: "previously certified" is the sum of what the issued interim certificates carried forward (gross less retention less previously certified, as issued, overrides included); the gross this period is the gross less the latest issued gross.
  - BD-613-26: one valuation at a time per contract (open, or raised with its certificate neither issued nor void); a valuation is opened on the agreed day of the month (the last day of a shorter month) or on a contractor's claim, after the last issued valuation; lists submitted after the valuation day go to the next cycle.
  - BD-613-27: the materials allowance reads the contract's own percentage first, then the organisation's default with its provenance; an item is valued only with all three answers yes and not temporary works; a failing line is shown, not valued.
  - BD-613-28: where retention reaches its cap, the cap is shared pro rata to what each tier would hold, half up, the main contractor's tier taking the rounding (the sources state no split; ASSUMED).
  - BD-613-29: the subsequent-certificate minimum is judged on the amount carried forward, the first on the gross (the sources state no measure); below it the valuation is held to the next cycle, with a reason.
  - BD-613-30 (BD-613-13 resolved): on an interim payment certificate the recommending officer is a representative whose delegation is in force on the day and whose scope includes valuation (RECOMMENDING_ACT, an exhaustive map; every other kind still resolves nobody and says so).
  - BD-613-31: before the contract is signed, every verified policy needs its premium receipt (a cover note is not evidence without it), the stricter reading.
  - BD-613-32: how many interim certificates may issue before the contract is signed is a setting (preExecutionInterimLimit with provenance), unset: none.
  - BD-613-33: preliminaries are a rule only at this step: the main contractor's works are valued as one figure to date.
  - BD-613-34: a retention release is paid through the next interim certificate (no separate release route); the first release needs the as-built handover and the configured first-release share (unset refuses), the balance the Certificate of Making Good Defects.
  - BD-613-35: nominated parties and assignees are totals only (B1): what is due on each certificate is keyed; the waterfall's allocations are derived from the recommended payment, never stored.
  - BD-613-36: only the keyed deductions (premiums and social security, wages, non-compliance, up to three others) may be overridden on an interim certificate, and only while it is a draft; every computed total is recomputed, never overridden; once a route is open the figures are frozen.
  - BD-613-37: a certificate's route never carries a committee step (refused before it opens, ROUTE_HAS_COMMITTEE); the sender may not decide it (FR-APR-006 applied to the sender); a route refused with no configured destination waits for the officer's choice before it can be sent again.
  - BD-613-38: the Certificate of Non-Completion routes on `non_completion` at the contract sum it pinned, and needs both its form and a routing sheet; the warning letter needs its form.
  - BD-613-39: the warning's response clock (LAD_WARNING_RESPONSE) is advisory and runs from posting by registered post, frozen with the letter; the damages rate is the pinned baseline's (the whole works' or the section's).
  - BD-613-40: an advance guarantee below the advance warns, it does not refuse; recoupment is by the schedule's certificate numbers.
  - BD-613-41: an interim certificate no valuation raised (an import's or an earlier build's) is counted in the sequence as the issue counts it, and refuses every later valuation by name (INTERIM_NOT_FROM_VALUATION): previously certified cannot be read from figures not in the valuation's layout. 6.14's demo_hct1 was the only one; it is now raised from interim valuation 1 (b794726, flagged 6.14).
  - BD-613-42: the issued adjustments of the contract sum are an empty list until step 4 (FR-PAD-003); the figure is on every certificate already, at nil.
  - BD-613-43: KIND_FIGURE_KEYS for the interim certificate stays empty: no form may yet merge a figure into its text (the figures print in the attached valuation); a question for 6.14 increment 2.
  - BD-613-44: the contractor's bank details are not captured before sign-in (6.15 OD-3 B): said on the Finance card, not checked.
  - BD-613-45: a certificate route's decider refusals read in the certificate's words (who sent it, its routed value, the day sent); found by the harness (d208aa6). A route not checked against the matrix says so on the certificate (92bbf4f, flagged 6.14; found by the text snapshot).
  - BD-613-46: the demo's payment figures are invented and ASSUMED (materials allowance 80%, issue clock 20 days, warning response 10 days; never the customer's); a contract past its date for completion, and with it the damages chain, an advance and a retention release on the demo, wait for step 5's back-dated chain (the screens show their empty states and refusals now).

A contract executed before 6.13 gets a one-off "Resolve the S.O." effective from the day it is recorded, never back-dated.
  - BD-613-10 (614-C4): the delegation letter opens no approval route (its block is the S.O.'s own), so 614-C4 moves to step 3 with the interim certificate, the first certificate that opens one.
  - BD-613-11 (who may be recorded): the S.O. is any holder of the office on the day or someone acting in it under a whole-office delegation (6.1); a representative is a delegate whose letter is issued on or before the day and not revoked by it (the revocation day is the first day it no longer holds) and whose scope lists the act; the special meeting's chair, early action, self-help and the S.O.'s own diary endorsement are the S.O.'s only; possession, programme approval and directions are the S.O.'s or the Principal Representative's; a superior officer's endorsement is recorded as named (no reporting lines yet).
  - BD-613-12: planned progress between two programme points is read on a straight line; In Delay compares exactly in integers, and exactly on the line is not below it (60.9% of 87% at 70% is not In Delay; 60.8% is). A lapsed date for completion with the work incomplete is In Delay whatever the percentage.
  - BD-613-13 (against accepted 6.14): the SO block is held by the S.O. office's holders on the day (and those acting in it); SO_REP by the Principal Representatives in force; RECOMMENDING_OFFICER resolves nobody until the interim certificate (step 3) says who recommends, and the block says so.
  - BD-613-14: possession freezes the start-of-work reading (BD-612-22) on the possession row and in the POSSESSION version's snapshot; recordPossession is the only ACTIVE writer and the lock-order scans name it.
  - BD-613-15: a delegation needs the S.O.'s recorded satisfaction with a note, a scope of at least one act, the contract type's letter variant and both forms set; one live delegation per person; never a representative delegating; it holds from the letter's certificate date; a void letter is not in force.
  - BD-613-16: one diary entry a day; an entry written after its day says why; missing days are shown, not blocking; a disagreement is stored and marked escalated to the S.O. (ASSUMED path), with no route.
  - BD-613-17: self-help records a PendingDeduction on the policy; deducting it is the interim certificate's (step 3).
  - BD-613-18: early action records its branches only, each saying where it opens (extensions of time and termination with Part B; liquidated damages with step 3's chain).
  - BD-613-19 (B1, OD-A8 A, ADR 0025): parties are totals only, recorded on an executed contract, with an optional sum, direct-payment answer and provenance; retired with a reason, never deleted.
  - BD-613-20: a handover pins the live versions of its set; PROGRAMME attachments open from execution, PIPE_LOG, AS_BUILT and DIARY_PHOTO once the contract is active.
  - BD-613-21 (OD-A7): the re-resolution switch is stored and shown; nothing writes RE_RESOLVED until an issued adjustment exists (step 4); unset warns on the Team tab and on every S.O. block.
  - BD-613-22: the demo's In Delay percentage is an invented 75 (never the customer's 70); the demo runs on demo_con3; the back-dated chain on a further contract waits for step 5.
  - BD-613-23: the supervision trail labels days in words and names its fields so the contract's History never reads them as codes (a party's type, a direction's text).
  - BD-613-24: possession judges the contract's state before the person recorded for it, so a contract not stamped says so first.
  - Existing tests changed: gate-sums-note.test.ts (as ruled, 64-C4-b); baseline-entry.test.ts (as ruled, 612-C1); lock-order.test.ts (614-C2 imports only; "nothing in 6.12 writes ACTIVE" now names recordPossession as the one writer, as ruled; the scans extended to the supervision acts and the demo writer list to its loader — stricter); worked-examples.test.ts and resolve.test.ts (input shape only, as ruled); form-use.test.ts (four delegation-form relations, faithful); screen-words.test.ts (the Documents tab's text as the facts changed: a delegation now raises its letters); crumbs.test.ts (one assertion added); certificate-scan.test.ts (the demo writer lists name the supervision demo's loader, which opens the letter pair as drafts — as the lists are designed to be extended). Verify on perolehan_verify: 62/62 through the real acts (setup edit: demo_con3 returned to stamped with nothing on site). Text snapshot: docs/notes/cloud-build/evidence/6.13A-run1-text-snapshot.diff (History reorderings among rows of the same instant, nothing dropped). Spec: FR-SUP-001–015, FR-APR-002, FR-MTD-012, FR-CRT-004, B1, OD-A7, OD-A8, ADR 0024 Amendment 1, ADR 0025, AGENTS.md rules 1–5.

R-10 eec1398 + c1ab7d6: an outsider's day, and the correction, are refused before the certificate was opened (BEFORE_OPENED) or before its route was approved (BEFORE_APPROVED) (verify 5, 7, 19–20). - 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · Ruling Q1 (BD-612-23) · Keep: a baseline correction refuses a value the award, the Appendix or the bill states, or a recorded rule derives; only ENTERED values (and the Contract Date by its own act) are corrected. Not allowed: an officer's figure over a stated value "with both shown", because it would rank an entered figure above the Letter of Acceptance and leave the award and the baseline disagreeing. Fix now: STATED_NOT_CORRECTED's words (EN+MS) say a stated value recorded wrongly cannot yet be corrected and stop pointing to variations (a variation changes the terms, it does not fix a record; AGENTS.md rule 2); a pinned sentence that changes is faithful to this ruling; ADR 0021 Amendment 1 records the narrowing of §1's CORRECTION and the intended exit. Carry 612-C1: a reasoned, audited correction of the award's recorded terms after issue (6.11's updateAwardTerms refuses after issue), writing a CORRECTION version sourced AWARD and shown "corrected from …"; home ruled at 6.13 planning (6.11 follow-up or 6.13 Part A); it must land before any certificate reads a baseline in production. Spec: FR-CON-014, ADR 0021 §1, §2, §4, OD-5 A, AGENTS.md rule 2.

- 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · BD-612-23..29 (fix-round decisions, for ruling) · BD-612-23 (R-3, supersedes BD-612-9's "any value"): a baseline correction refuses a group the frozen inputs state (the Letter of Acceptance, the Appendix, the bill) or a recorded rule derives, naming it, rather than keeping the original source beside a corrected figure: ADR 0021 puts the award over the Appendix over the bill and keys only what nothing states, so an officer's correction over a stated value would rank an entered figure above the award; a change to the terms is 6.13's (variations and adjustments) and writes its own version. Groups entered at baseline are corrected as before, ENTERED with the provenance and reason. BD-612-24: an arithmetical correction's tendered and corrected figures are priced bid figures, SULIT as 6.8's correctedPrice is, registered as tenderedLineAmount / correctedLineAmount (specific keys, not "tendered"/"corrected"); the check's variance in the completion trail is not registered (it is the difference OD-3 A says is shown beside the sum on the Overview); for ruling whether it should be. BD-612-25 (S-1): numberedOn is the organisation's day as stored by the issue act, so the issue year is read from the stored day (dayOf on it would move a west-of-UTC day back); the year note and the December/January flag apply to SERIES numbers only, since an indent's own number states no issue year. BD-612-26 (R-6): a retired row is not re-dated by retiring it again (refused, naming the day); "retires from" shows a scheduled retirement. BD-612-27: the legal comments a return stands on are those attached from the review's sending to its decision (the window decideLegal accepts), by version, whether or not comments were also typed; attachNewVersion still moves the pin, as for every other reliance. BD-612-28 (M-1): a Contract Date correction on a contract executed before this round reads the signature days from the signature rows (single writer, never updated). BD-612-29 (S-2): the shared fix wraps every list badge wider than its column; /projects' three longer statuses now take two lines in its 200px column at 1280 and 1440 rather than any list's width changing. Spec: as above.

- 2026-10-03 · 6.12 · BD-612-1..22 (build decisions, for ruling) · BD-612-1 (step 0): §0.1 re-checked against the as-built code. 6.4's Appendix is read through frozenTenderOf and the approved pack's Appendix (first and subsequent interim minimums, days to honour, sections); 6.2's prime cost and provisional sums are the totals frozen when the bill the approved pack pinned was finalised; the first-interim floor and percentage are TenderDocumentPolicy.firstCertificateFloor/Pct; the LAD a day is 6.4's ladDaily (half up). Pre-6.4 exercises (all three demo contracts) have none of these and key them (OD-5). No divergence needing a re-ruling. BD-612-2: starting preparation refuses, naming each, where a setting formation cannot do without is unset (legal office, the agreement's signatory condition, the controlled-copy stamp, at least one copy recipient, the basis's "let on a bill" answer, its document parts); so no contract ever holds a frozen-unset rule it cannot leave. The front-loading ratio may be unset: the check is then the officer's judgement, said so. BD-612-3: which checks a basis takes is read from 6.2's ContractBasis.requiresBq (true: rate uniformity; false: the Schedule of Rates' adjustment; arithmetic and front loading always), frozen at the start; no new column. A services basis with no schedule records that check complete with a note. BD-612-4: front loading lists rates strictly above the frozen multiple; a rate exactly at it is listed apart "for judgement" (no inclusive switch is configured, so neither reading is chosen); a note is required whenever anything is listed or no ratio is set; read exactly in integers. BD-612-5: the contract goes to the legal office only with the assembly, reconciliation and checks complete; they are frozen while it is there and once endorsed, and open again when it is returned. BD-612-6: the contractor's signature needs what the RSAJ one needs before it (legally endorsed, the gate met, the resolution live): FR-CON-008 says the signature action. BD-612-7: confirming the baseline needs the readiness gate as 6.11 judges it (verified, stricter than FR-PAY-014's "lodged"). BD-612-8: "first ready to sign" for the Contract Date correction is the latest of the endorsement day, the instrument's receipt, each verification and the WC check day, and the resolution's attaching day (the organisation's day), frozen with the signature. BD-612-9: a baseline correction may correct any value (its source becomes ENTERED, with the officer's provenance and the reason); the Contract Date is corrected only by its own act. BD-612-10: a correction or rate line can be taken off, with a reason, only while its check is incomplete (the trail keeps it). BD-612-11: the estimate rate is SULIT (registered as estimateRate): shown with the chip, kept out of the trail. BD-612-12: the copy recipients and stamp words are frozen at preparation, not at issue; the consulting-engineer answer locks once a copy is issued. BD-612-13: the Configuration screen offers the signatory condition as the award_signatory rows that name no instrument, by their labels; no code is shown. BD-612-14: the briefing may be recorded from legal endorsement onwards; a signed contract without one shows a warning and nothing refuses (a Should). BD-612-15: the WC check is recorded on a verified WC policy on an uncovered-wages award, before execution, once. BD-612-16: /projects derives "awaiting conditions" and "ready to sign" for at most 200 legally endorsed contracts. BD-612-17: the contract's History reads its own rows and every row written under "Contract <number> ·". BD-612-18: the demo forms the three contracts 6.11 numbered (con1 returned once, con2 endorsed and awaiting conditions, con3 executed 30 Sep, stamped and distributed), each act judged by the product's own rule and dated on or after its issue, rather than three new back-dated awards (con10–12): a contract executed in March for 6.13's demo is 6.13's to load. The rulings' demo_ai100–119, mt15–19 and d50–59 were grepped free and are not needed; the demo_k family is used without the "demo_kc" sub-prefix. BD-612-19: spec IDs stay in comments, as on every other screen. BD-612-20: the readiness gate's words move to one shared list the award and the contract both render (flagged, 6.11). BD-612-21: carry 64-C4-b (the award's own insurance terms) is not in the approved 6.12 plan and is not built; it is carried, for ruling where it belongs (a 6.11 follow-up or 6.13 Part A). BD-612-22: the type's startRequiresSecurities behaviour is not read at signing: the readiness gate is the award's own bond decision (bond required, or not with its reason) and the configured insurance requirements, which already bind every signature; a type that starts without securities is a 6.13 possession question. Spec: as above.

- 2026-10-02 · 6.15a · BD-615-2..17 (build decisions, for ruling) · BD-615-2: entity types and countries are the register's own list (SupplierListValue), not classification taxonomies (step 0: a supplier list would appear as an exercise classification). BD-615-3: a route names its supplier record from the route (ApprovalInstance.supplierApplicationId, standingRequestId, specialPermissionId; one CHECK says a SUPPLIER route names exactly one, any other none), not the plan's approvalInstanceId @unique on the record: a refused route leaves the matter open to be sent again, so a record may carry several routes; a blacklisting is asked for as a StandingRequest whose route must approve before the StandingEvent is recorded from it (the plan's event.approvalInstanceId could not exist before its route). BD-615-4: a supplier route's offices are held organisation-wide (supplierHolders); a delegation with a value ceiling is not counted (a matter with no value cannot be judged against a ceiling); a committee step on a supplier rule is refused (HAS_COMMITTEE_STEP) and the three domains are never tabled. BD-615-5: a document is verified only with its scan attached (SCAN_REQUIRED). BD-615-6: a contact's name, designation, email and telephone are registered SULIT fields (History chips them, the audit export withholds them) while the screens carry "not restricted until sign-in" (condition 2). BD-615-7: eligibility reads NOT_ASSESSED, never PASS, where 6.4 states a requirement's qualifiers in words only (no structured needs reach a requirement yet; the comparison by configured rank, any-of and all-of is built and tested in src/lib/suppliers/eligibility.ts, how structured needs reach a requirement is held for ruling with BD-615-1), where no document type records the body, where the exercise does not state whether it is restricted, and where the lapse rule is unset with a mandatory document expired. BD-615-8: the window check counts the verified chain together (a verified renewal starting the day after its predecessor ends carries the cover on); a renewal lodged and not verified makes a lapse inside the window NOT_ASSESSED, not FAIL. BD-615-9: StandingEvent.endsOn is the last blacklisted day (inclusive; free from the day after, BD-DS-10 arithmetic); a lift is its own request on supplier_blacklist (cond BLACKLIST_LIFT); REGISTRATION_CANCELLED is in the vocabulary and read by standing and the sale check, but no a2 act writes it (6.13 Part B or later). BD-615-10: the reinstatement checklist is one line per document the suspension was imposed for, each ticked as seen verified (no configured checklist; the rule is the documents). BD-615-11: at a sale, a linked party's registration and suspension checks are the register's (the officer's attestation is not used for it); a cancelled registration fails the registration check, a suspension (by hand or on lapse) or blacklisting the suspension check; the eligibility verdict is frozen on the sale and does not itself refuse it. BD-615-12: OD-6's consequence reuses ELIMINATE / REPORT; NOT_ASSESSED at an opening is shown, not a ground; the consequence is read from the register's policy until the opening is finalised and frozen then in the opening's policySnapshot (ineligible), audited. BD-615-13: a special permission needs the party linked (it is read by the supplier's eligibility); the notice's offer is a new window item (ExerciseWindow.specialPermissionOffered, null = not stated, which refuses); granted at once where only bodies outside the organisation grant it, after its route where the organisation's own approval is asked; it relaxes the grade or the field codes only. BD-615-14: the register's standing rules are their own act and audit label (Supplier register · standing rules), apart from the verification notice; reminder offsets come from the NotificationRule supplier.document.expiring, shown and never sent. BD-615-15: standing is never stored; the register list and the record's header show the derived standing (OD-7's exact wording where it applies). BD-615-16: demo ids beyond the plan's list, each grepped free first: demo_vnd7, demo_vapp7/7r, demo_vct7, demo_ai127/128 (vnd7's registration and pending renewal), demo_vscan*, demo_vdoc*, demo_n21 (series), demo_f6 (format), demo_nr7 (reminder rule), demo_rule120–122, demo_vsr1 (blacklisting request); 6.5's demo sx18 notice offers special permission (own flagged commit). BD-615-17: an award to a supplier who is suspended or blacklisted is not refused by 6.11 in a2 (deferred: the plan names the sale and the opening hooks only). Spec: FR-VND-001–004, 007–011, FR-ADV-010, 017, FR-BID-008 (paper), FR-TRM-017, FR-AWD-009, INT-03, OD-1..OD-10, conditions 1–6, AGENTS.md rules 1, 2 and 5.

- 2026-10-02 · Phase 2 close-out · 68-C5 (storedDay on instants), presented for review. The carry's six sites were examined first: ApprovalInstance.openedAt is not an instant. Every act that opens a route writes the organisation's day at midnight UTC (dayToDate(today); the intake writes the received trail's first decision day; openSuccessor is given dayToDate(today)), and all 45 rows in perolehan were at midnight. storedDay(inst.openedAt) in recordNegotiationDecision (meeting-actions.ts:249), decideAppointmentStep (awards/actions.ts:592), recordNameDecision (sourcing/actions.ts:628), recordDecision (requisitions/actions.ts:1248), recordWindowChangeDecision (window/change-actions.ts:331) and recordTenderingDecision (documents/actions.ts:757) is therefore correct in every zone; replacing it with dayIn(openedAt, zone), as the carry asked, would move the opened day back a day west of UTC (midnight UTC is 20:00 the evening before in New York). Not changed; proved instead by dd00fa3 (flagged against accepted 6.1, 6.3, 6.4, 6.5, 6.8, 6.11; source scan: every approvalInstance.create writes openedAt from the organisation's day, the list of writers is complete, successors open on dayToDate(today), the six acts and the Approval tab read it with storedDay; mutation-checked). (A first attempt that switched meeting-actions.ts to the zone's day was dropped before review, unpushed, for this reason.) The whole-src grep then found real instants read as UTC days, each fixed in its own flagged commit with new tests: 279b9b0 dayOf(instant, timeZone), the organisation's day with no UTC fallback (null with no zone); 4062931 (6.2) a bill version's finalisedAt and a panel verification's clearedAt (new Date()) shown with formatOrgDay; 84e04c9 (6.4) the assurance confirmation's pack-started day (TenderPack.createdAt) fell back to its UTC day with no zone, now dayOf and refused TIME_ZONE_NOT_SET; 1a47cca (6.8) the evaluation's startedAt, the recommendation's formedAt and the report's issuedAt read with dayIn (UTC fallback) now dayOf, and renderProposalPaper, which printed the report's issued day into the frozen Kertas Cadangan with no refusal when the zone had been cleared after the report issued, now refuses TIME_ZONE_NOT_SET; fd3da01 (6.10) Meeting.minutedAt and CommitteeDecision.withdrawnAt passed to the trail as Dates (renderValue writes their UTC day) now written with formatOrgDateTime. Existing test changed (flagged, stricter): evaluation/org-day.test.ts's R-4 source scan requires dayOf where it required dayIn, and that dayIn is not used on those instants; its behaviour cases unchanged. Listed, not changed: storedDay/ISO-slice of day columns (dates typed or written with dayToDate) everywhere else; Meeting.minutedAt read with formatOrgDateTime on the sitting page (correct, an instant); the window, opening, bids and estimate dayIn calls are all guarded on a set zone. Spec: NFR-AUD-005, ruling §9.5, BD-32, AGENTS.md rule 2.

- 2026-10-02 · Phase 2 close-out · 68-C3 (award gate and the quotation ceiling), presented for review. bb38d5e (flagged against accepted 6.11 and 6.8): loadEvaluationGate reads the exercise's frozen regime, boundaryValue and boundaryOperator (ceilingOf) onto the issued recommendation; evaluationFindings asks awardCeilingFault, the same ceilingExclusion rule 6.8 Q1 uses, whenever the awarded value differs from the recommendation: above the ceiling VALUE_ABOVE_CEILING names the boundary and which side an exact value falls (GT still a quotation, GTE a tender); a quotation whose boundary was never recorded blocks as BOUNDARY_NOT_RECORDED; a tender has no ceiling here; a value equal to the recommendation is not asked again (Q1 judged it when formed and at the report). Opening, editing, submission and issue all read it through evaluationFindings; the award form shows it under Awarded value. The ceiling is optional in the pure gate (absent: not asked, BD-68-6) and the one producer always states it (source scan). New tests only (ceiling-gate.test.ts, worked example): GT and GTE at the boundary and one sen above, tender never, boundary unset, equal value not asked, wording without codes. Spec: FR-EVL-016, FR-EVL-019, E-9, 6.8 Q1, AGENTS.md rules 1 and 2.

New tests only (whole-path.test.ts); panel-unit.test.ts's prefix match unchanged. Spec: FR-CTE-011, C-6, R-6.

- 2026-10-02 · 6.8 · Build decisions (BD-68-1..17), for ruling. 1 Step 0 (plan §0.1): names used as built — evaluationSet, the frozen schedule's benchmark as the only estimate read, consentPartyId, publishFaults' one more input, the negotiation domain in the one route splitter; no rule changed. 2 EvaluationState is OPEN, ISSUED, SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED (no SUBMITTED: a stage's submission is its own row); Evaluation.policySnapshot freezes the score's places and their provenance at the start (E-5), the 6.6/6.7 freeze pattern. 3 Lock order extended at its end (requisition → exercise → window → openings → envelopes/bids → evaluations); the two acts that come from a sitting lock sitting → evaluations, never the exercise, so no two acts take the same locks in opposite orders (lock.ts). 4 The pin is stamped with the publication's own clock read and guarded on evaluationModelPinnedAt null (a second pin refuses CHANGED); a published or cancelled exercise takes no model choice. 5 The E-9 gate sits in evaluationFindings, and exerciseOpenForAward accepts every status past the opening, so an exercise published before models existed reads NEVER_EVALUATED (naming cancel and replace) rather than a status refusal; no C-8 test was rewritten. 6 The evaluation input is optional in the pure award gates (old fixtures unchanged); the one producer, awardFacts, always states it, held by a source scan (hooks.test.ts). 7 A draft award's edit asks only the faults a field can fix (fieldsOnly); the rest are judged at submission. 8 Configuration: children are audited against their model's id; one draft per model at a time; retiring keeps every pinned snapshot; a draft never issued may be discarded with a reason. 9 Which side of a pass mark or a band edge an exact value falls on is stated for each one; unset blocks the save or the issue, never defaults (AGENTS.md boundaryInclusive pattern). 10 The loop-back never refuses the committee's outcome: an unknown or legacy key, a TERMINAL outcome or an exercise not published through the window records the outcome and says why no version opens. 11 No inputs for a bid excluded or out of the running at the stage reading them (runningFaults, shared with assessAspect); found by the verify run. 12 The recommendation is refused while a negotiation is still with approval (NEGOTIATION_PENDING, the report's code): formed first, an approved negotiation could never be held; found by the verify run. 13 Demo ids: attendance demo_at64–67 (E-12 named at60–63, already 6.6's); document counter from 110; sx16's evaluation stamped 145 to 100 minutes before now, before demo_aw7 (90); demo_sx21 and demo_c7 per 67-C3. 14 The demo's "Return for re-clarification" (demo_nao1, 6.10's) keeps its legacy free-text stage "evaluation", so Configuration shows the legacy warning and demo_d43 stays PENDING (plan §6); the verify run re-keys it through Configuration and shows the loop. 15 The record's "Evaluation runs outside the portal" banner removed and the new exercise's "Sourcing runs outside the portal for now" subtitle reworded, both false once built (flagged 6.7, 6.3). 16 The demo reads an exercise's closing through loadExercise, not its own select, so the X-2 scan (closing-writers.test.ts) stays as accepted. 17 6.5's publications table scrolls inside its own frame at 1280 (as on demo_sx18 before 6.8); not changed. Questions for RSAJ: D1 (a model per method in use), OD-5, OD-10. Spec: FR-EVL-001–020, FR-CTE-007, 009, 013, E-1..E-13, OD-1..OD-10.
